# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

## CayleyPy Development 3: Growth computations

Data CardCode (329)Discussion (0)Suggestions (0)

## About Dataset

# CaleyPy

Artificial intelligence based library for studying groups, graphs, languages with focus on Cayley graphs.

Dataset with development, experiments and testing notebooks. Dataset 3 - that part devoted to growth computations.

Github: https://github.com/cayleypy/cayleypy/tree/main

Papers:
https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18663
https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13266


## Overview

This what we plan to support:

  * Machine learning based methods to decompose given element of the group into product of generators.
  * Machine learning based methods to approximate the word metric on graphs
  * Efficient implementations of other algorithms:
** BFS from given state (growth function, adjacency matrix, last layers).
** Efficient path finding (beam search).
** Efficient computing on CPU, GPU, TPU (with JAX), usable on Kaggle.
  * Further
** Hamiltonian paths search, spectrum analysis, etc…

## Development

To start development, clone the repository, then run:
    
    `pip install -r requirements.txt
    pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
    `
    
    [Button: content_copy]

Before committing, run these checks:
    
    `pycodestyle --max-line-length=120 ./cayleypy 
    pytest 
    `
    
    [Button: content_copy]

citeturn1search0 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last month;     `pip install git+https://github.com/cayleypy/cayleypy ...     `git clone https://github.com/cayleypy/cayleypy.git

# CayleyPy

AI-based library to work with extremely large graphs.
Supporting: Cayley graphs, Schreier coset graphs, more to be added.

## Overview

Extremely large graphs (e.g. googol size, ~10^{100} nodes) cannot be approached in a usual way, it is impossible neither to create, neither to store them by standard methods.

Typically such graphs arise as state-transition graphs. For chess, Go or any other games - nodes of the graphs are positions, edges correspond to moves between them. For Rubik's cube - nodes are configurations, edges corresponds to configurations different by single moves.

The most simple and clear examples of such graphs - are Cayley graphs in mathematics. (and Schreier coset graphs ). Initial developments will focus on these graphs, supporting other types later.

We plan to support:

  * ML/RL methods for pathfinding
  * Estimation of diameters and growths
  * Embeddings
  * Efficient BFS for small subgraphs
  * Efficient random walks generation
  * Efficient Beam Search
  * Hamiltonian paths finding
  * Efficient computing on CPU, GPU, TPU (with JAX), usable on Kaggle.
  * Etc.

Mathematical applications:

  * Estimation of diameters and growths
  * Approximation of the word metrics and diffusion distance
  * Estimation of the mixing time for random walks of different types
  * BFS from given state (growth function, adjacency matrix, last layers).
  * Library of graphs and generators (LRX, TopSpin, Rubik Cubes, wreath, globe etc., see here).
  * Library of datasets with solutions to some problems (e.g. growth functions like here).

## Examples

See the following Kaggle notebooks for examples of library usage:

# Kaggle CLI Documentation

Welcome to the Kaggle CLI documentation. This guide provides detailed information on how to use the Kaggle command-line interface to interact with Kaggle's platform.

## Installation

Note: Ensure you have Python 3.11+ and the package manager `pip` installed.

Install the `kaggle` package with pip:
    
    pip install kaggle

If you run into a `Command kaggle not found` error, ensure that your Python executable scripts are in your $PATH. For a local user install on Linux, the default location is `~/.local/bin`. On Windows, the default location is `$PYTHON_HOME/Scripts`.

## Authentication

First, you will need a Kaggle account. You can sign up here.

After login, you can download your Kaggle API credentials at https://www.kaggle.com/settings/api by clicking on the "Generate New Token" button under the "API" section.

### Option 1: OAuth

Obtains access credentials for your Kaggle user accounts via a web-based authrorization flow.
    
    kaggle auth login

### Option 2: Environment variable
    
    export KAGGLE_API_TOKEN=xxxxxxxxxxxxxx # Copied from the settings UI

### Option 3: API token file

Store your Kaggle API token obtained from your Kaggle account API tokens settings p<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="5105" />Years of Competitions, Community and Data Science Innovation (https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06304)

# CayleyPy Development 3: Growth computations

Effective growth computations for Cayley and Schreier coset graphs

Image

## CayleyPy Development 3: Growth computations

Data CardCode (329)Discussion (0)Suggestions (0)

## About Dataset

# CaleyPy

Artificial intelligence based library for studying groups, graphs, languages with focus on Cayley graphs.

Dataset with development, experiments and testing notebooks. Dataset 3 - that part devoted to growth computations.

Github: https://github.com/cayleypy/cayleypy/tree/main

Papers:
https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.18663
https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.13266


## Overview

This what we plan to support:

  * Machine learning based methods to decompose given element of the group into product of generators.
  * Machine learning based methods to approximate the word metric on graphs
  * Efficient implementations of other algorithms:
** BFS from given state (growth function, adjacency matrix, last layers).
** Efficient path finding (beam search).
** Efficient computing on CPU, GPU, TPU (with JAX), usable on Kaggle.
  * Further
** Hamiltonian paths search, spectrum analysis, etc…

## Development

To start development, clone the repository, then run:
    
    `pip install -r requirements.txt
    pip install -r requirements-dev.txt
    `
    
    [Button: content_copy]

Before committing, run these checks:
    
    `pycodestyle --max-line-length=120 ./cayleypy 
    pytest 
    `
    
    [Button: content_copy]

## Dataset Overview

Dataset Contains the Following:

  * Fixtures and Scores: `fixtures.csv`
  * Player possession stats: `player_possession_stats.csv`
  * Team possession stats: `team_possession_stats.csv`
  * Player Salaries: `player_salaries.csv`
  * Team salaries: `team_salary.csv`
  * Basic player stats: `player_stats.csv`
  * Basic Team stats: `team_stats.csv`
  * League Standings: `standings.csv`

Position Glossary:

  * `GK`: Goalkeepers
  * `DF`: Defenders
  * `MF`: Midfielders
  * `FW`: Forwards
  * `FB`: Fullbacks
  * `LB`: Left Backs
  * `RB`: Right Backs
  * `CB`: Center Backs
  * `DM`: Defensive Midfielders
  * `CM`: Central Midfielders
  * `LM`: Left Midfielders
  * `RM`: Right Midfielders
  * `WM`: Wide Midfielders
  * `LW`: Left Wingers
  * `RW`: Right Wingers
  * `AM`: Attacking Midfielders


## `fixtures.csv`

Features:

  * `week`: week of match
  * `Day`: weekday of match
  * `Date`: date of match
  * `Time`: time of kickoff
  * `Home`: home team
  * `HomeScore`: home team score
  * `Away`: away team
  * `AwayScore`: away team score
  * `Attendance`: match attendance
*`Venue`: stadium
  * `Referee`: head official

citeturn1search0 [wordlim: 200] Crawled: last month;     `pip install git+https://github.com/cayleypy/cayleypy ...     `git clone https://github.com/cayleypy/cayleypy.git

# CayleyPy

AI-based library to work with extremely large graphs.
Supporting: Cayley graphs, Schreier coset graphs, more to be added.

## Overview

Extremely large graphs (e.g. googol size, ~10^{100} nodes) cannot be approached in a usual way, it is impossible neither to create, neither to store them by standard methods.

Typically such graphs arise as state-transition graphs. For chess, Go or any other games - nodes of the graphs are positions, edges correspond to moves between them. For Rubik's cube - nodes are configurations, edges corresponds to configurations different by single moves.

The most simple and clear examples of such graphs - are Cayley graphs in mathematics. (and Schreier coset graphs ). Initial developments will focus on these graphs, supporting other types later.

We plan to support:

  * ML/RL methods for pathfinding
  * Estimation of diameters and growths
  * Embeddings
  * Efficient BFS for small subgraphs
  * Efficient random walks generation
  * Efficient Beam Search
  * Hamiltonian paths finding
  * Efficient computing on CPU, GPU, TPU (with JAX), usable on Kaggle.
  * Etc.

Mathematical applications:

  * Estimation of diameters and growths
  * Approximation of the word metrics and diffusion distance
  * Estimation of the mixing time for random walks of different types
  * BFS from given state (growth function, adjacency matrix, last layers).
  * Library of graphs and generators (LRX, TopSpin, Rubik Cubes, wreath, globe etc., see here).
  * Library of datasets with solutions to some problems (e.g. growth functions like here).

## Examples

See the following Kaggle notebooks for examples of library usage:

# Kaggle CLI Documentation

Welcome to the Kaggle CLI documentation. This guide provides detailed information on how to use the Kaggle command-line interface to interact with Kaggle's platform.

## Installation

Note: Ensure you have Python 3.11+ and the package manager `pip` installed.

Install the `kaggle` package with pip:
    
    pip install kaggle

If you run into a `Command kaggle not found` error, ensure that your Python executable scripts are in your $PATH. For a local user install on Linux, the default location is `~/.local/bin`. On Windows, the default location is `$PYTHON_HOME/Scripts`.

## Authentication

First, you will need a Kaggle account. You can sign up here.

After login, you can download your Kaggle API credentials at https://www.kaggle.com/settings/api by clicking on the "Generate New Token" button under the "API" section.

### Option 1: OAuth

Obtains access credentials for your Kaggle user accounts via a web-based authrorization flow.
    
    kaggle auth login

### Option 2: Environment variable
    
    export KAGGLE_API_TOKEN=xxxxxxxxxxxxxx # Copied from the settings UI

### Option 3: API token file

Store your Kaggle API token obtained from your Kaggle account API tokens settings p<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="5105" />Years of Competitions, Community and Data Science Innovation (https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.06304)

import json
with open("cfg.json", "w") as f:
   json.dump(cfg, f)
CELL 9
# Load data to solve and sample submit
CELL 10
### Some utilities ###

def parse_initial_state(initial_state_str: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Parse a comma-separated puzzle state string into an integer array."""
    return np.array([int(x) for x in initial_state_str.split(",")], dtype=int)


def parse_path(path_str: str) -> list[str]:
    """Parse a dot-separated generator path string into generator names."""
    return list(path_str.split("."))


def check_path(
    path_to_check: Sequence[str],
    initial_state: np.ndarray,
    central_state: np.ndarray,
    generators: dict[str, np.ndarray],
) -> bool:
    """Return whether applying a sequence of generator names reaches the central state."""
    state = initial_state.copy()
    for gen_name in path_to_check:
        gen = generators[gen_name]
        state = state[gen]
    return bool(np.all(state == central_state))


### Loading competition inputs ###


INPUTS_DIR = Path(input_dir_name)

with open(INPUTS_DIR/"puzzle_info.json", "r") as file:
    puzzle_info = json.load(file)


central_state = np.array(puzzle_info["central_state"])
generators = {k: np.array(v) for k, v in puzzle_info["generators"].items()}


df_test = pd.read_csv(INPUTS_DIR/"test.csv", index_col = "initial_state_id")
df_sample_submission = pd.read_csv(INPUTS_DIR/"sample_submission.csv", index_col = "initial_state_id")

display(df_test.head(2) )
print(' len( central_state ):', len( central_state )    )
df_sample_submission.head(20)

CELL 12
### Preparing CayleyPy graph ###

gens_names = list(generators.keys())
graph_def = CayleyGraphDef.create(
    generators = [generators[x] for x in gens_names],
    generator_names = gens_names,
    central_state = central_state
)

# Important: small batch_size may lead to significant slowing down the beam search, e.g. values like 2**8, but potentially better RAM utilization.

std::vector<std::string> load_p900_move_names(const std::filesystem::path& path) {
    const std::string text = read_text_file(path);
    std::size_t pos = text.find("\"names\"");
    const bool names_format = pos != std::string::npos;
    if (names_format) {
        pos = text.find('[', pos);
        if (pos == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_error("generator json malformed names");
        }
    } else {
        pos = text.find("\"generators\"");
        if (pos == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_erro<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="44" />      }
    }
    std::vector<std::string> names;
    while (names.size() < MOVE_COUNT) {
        const std::size_t begin = text.find('"', pos + 1);
        if (begin == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_error("p900 generator json missing move name");
        }
        const std::size_t end = text.find('"', begin + 1);
        if (end == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_error("p900 generator json malformed move name");
        }
        names.push_back(text.substr(begin + 1, end - begin - 1));
        pos = end;
        if (!names_format) {
            const std::size_t colon = text.find(':', pos);
            const std::size_t array = text.find('[', pos);
            if (colon == std::string::npos || array == std::string::npos || colon > array) {
                throw std::runtime_error("generator json malformed generator entry");
            }
            pos = array;
        }
    }
    return names;
}

State128 load_central_state(const std::filesystem::path& path) {
    const std::string text = read_text_file(path);
    std::size_t pos = text.find("\"central_state\"");
    if (pos == std::string::npos) {
        throw std::runtime_error("puzzle info json missing central_state");
    }
    pos = text.find('[', pos);
    if (pos == std::string::npos) {
        throw std::runtime_error("puzzle info json malformed central_state");
    }
    State128 state{};
    for (std::uint32_t p = 0; p < STATE_LEN; ++p) {
        state.v[p] = static_cast<std::uint8_t>(parse_next_u32(text, pos, "central_state"));
    }
    for (std::uint32_t p = STATE_LEN; p < STATE_STORAGE_LEN; ++p) {
        state.v[p] = 0;
    }
    return state;
}

class PilgrimAttnRes(nn.Module):
    """MLP distance model with optional residual or attention-residual blocks."""

    def __init__(self, config: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.dtype = torch.float32
        self.state_size = config['state_size']
        self.num_classes = config['num_classes']
        self.hd1 = config['hd1']
        self.hd2 = config['hd2']
        self.nrd = config['nrd']
        self.n_attn_blocks = config.get('n_attn_blocks', 0)
        self.block_type = config.get('block_type', 'residual')
        self.z_add = 0
        self.output_dim = 1

        state_size = self.state_size
        num_classes = self.num_classes
        hd1 = self.hd1
        hd2 = self.hd2
        dropout_rate = config['dropout_rate']

        self.input_layer = nn.Linear(state_size * num_classes, hd1)
        self.bn1 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hd1)
        self.relu = nn.ReLU()
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout_rate)

        if hd2 > 0:
            self.hidden_layer = nn.Linear(hd1, hd2)
            self.bn2 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hd2)
            hidden_dim_for_output = hd2
        else:
            self.hidden_layer = None
            self.bn2 = None
            hidden_dim_for_output = hd1

        if self.block_type == 'residual':
            self.residual_blocks = nn.ModuleList(
                [ResidualBlock(hd2, dropout_rate) for _ in range(self.nrd)]
            ) if self.nrd > 0 and hd2 > 0 else None
        elif self.block_type == 'attn_res':
            self.residual_blocks = nn.ModuleList(
                [AttnResidualBlock(hd2, dropout_rate) for _ in range(self.n_attn_blocks)]
            ) if self.n_attn_blocks > 0 and hd2 > 0 else None
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown block_type: {self.block_type}")

        self.output_layer = nn.Linear(hidden_dim_for_output, self.output_dim)

    def forward(self, z: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        """Predict distances for encoded puzzle states."""
        x = F.one_hot(z.long() + self.z_add, num_classes=self.num_classes).view(z.size(0), -1).to(self.dtype)

        x = self.input_layer(x)
        x = self.bn1(x)
        x = self.relu(x)
        x = self.dropout(x)

        if self.hidden_layer is not None:
            x = self.hidden_layer(x)
            x = self.bn2(x)
            x = self.relu(x)
            x = self.dropout(x)

        if self.residual_blocks is not None:
            if self.block_type == 'residual':
                for block in self.residual_blocks:
                    x = block(x)
            elif self.block_type == 'attn_res':
                prev_outputs = []
                for block in self.residual_blocks:
                    x = block(x, prev_outputs)
                    prev_outputs.append(x)

        x = self.output_layer(x)
        return x.flatten()


class ResidualBlock(nn.Module):
    """Two-layer residual MLP block with batch normalization and dropout."""

    def __init__(self, hidden_dim: int, dropout_rate: float = 0.1) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim)
        self.bn1 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hidden_dim)
        self.relu = nn.ReLU()
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout_rate)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim)
        self.bn2 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hidden_dim)

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        """Apply a residual feed-forward update to a hidden representation."""
        residual = x
        out = self.fc1(x)
        out = self.bn1(out)
        out = self.relu(out)
        out = self.dropout(out)
        out = self.fc2(out)
        out = self.bn2(out)
        out = out + residual
        out = self.relu(out)
        return out

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Export Stream1 PyTorch weights into runtime weight files."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
from typing import Dict, Literal, Tuple

import torch


TensorDict = Dict[str, torch.Tensor]
ExportDType = Literal["fp16", "bf16"]


def unwrap_state_dict(obj) -> TensorDict:
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        for key in ("state_dict", "model_state_dict", "model", "net", "module"):
            value = obj.get(key)
            if isinstance(value, dict):
                return value
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return obj
    raise TypeError(f"unsupported checkpoint root type: {type(obj).__name__}")


def strip_orig_mod(sd: TensorDict) -> TensorDict:
    return {(key[len("_orig_mod."):] if key.startswith("_orig_mod.") else key): value for key, value in sd.items()}


def fold_linear_bn(sd: TensorDict, linear: str, bn: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    gamma = sd[f"{bn}.weight"].detach().float()
    beta = sd[f"{bn}.bias"].detach().float()
    mean = sd[f"{bn}.running_mean"].detach().float()
    var = sd[f"{bn}.running_var"].detach().float()
    scale = gamma / torch.sqrt(var + 1e-5)
    return weight * scale[:, None], (bias - mean) * scale + beta


def linear_weight_bias(sd: TensorDict, linear: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    return weight, bias


def tensor_bytes(tensor: torch.Tensor, dty<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1319" />ed as position-class table",
        "source_weights": str(weights_path),
    }
    (out_dir / "manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(
        "stream1_export_done"
        f" out_dir={out_dir}"
        f" hd1={hd1_padded}"
        f" hd2={hd2_padded}"
        f" original_hd1={hd1}"
        f" original_hd2={hd2}"
        f" nrd={residual_count}"
        f" output_dim={output_dim}"
    )


def infer_resmlp_hidden_dims(sd: TensorDict) -> tuple[int, int, int, int, int]:
    embed_rows, embed_dim = sd["embedding.weight"].shape
    hidden1, flattened = sd["input_stack.0.weight"].shape
    hidden2, hidden1_in = sd["input_stack.3.weight"].shape
    output_dim, output_in = sd["head.weight"].shape
    if hidden1_in != hidden1 or output_in != hidden2:
        raise ValueError("ResMLPDistance hidden shape mismatch")
    if flattened % embed_dim != 0:
        raise ValueError(f"flattened input {flattened} is not divisible by embed_dim {embed_dim}")
    return int(embed_rows), int(embed_dim), int(hidden1), int(hidden2), int(output_dim)


def infer_resmlp_residual_count(sd: TensorDict) -> int:
    blocks = set()
    pattern = re.compile(r"^res_blocks\.(\d+)\.lin1\.weight$")
    for key in sd:
        match = pattern.match(key)
        if match:
            blocks.add(int(match.group(1)))
    if not blocks:
        raise ValueError("no res_blocks.*.lin1.weight tensors found")
    expected = set(range(max(blocks) + 1))
    if blocks != expected:
        raise ValueError(f"res block indices are not contiguous: found={sorted(blocks)}")
    return max(blocks) + 1


def linear_ln(sd: TensorDict, linear: str, ln: str) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight, bias = linear_weight_bias(sd, linear)
    gamma = sd[f"{ln}.weight"].detach().float()
    beta = sd[f"{ln}.bias"].detach().float()
    return weight, bias, gamma, beta

        write_vec(out_dir / f"{out_prefix}_fc2_ln_gamma{suffix}", gamma, dtype)
        write_vec(out_dir / f"{out_prefix}_fc2_ln_beta{suffix}", beta, dtype)

    weight, bias = linear_weight_bias(sd, "head")
    write_hxk(out_dir / f"output_weight_hxk{suffix}", weight, dtype)
    write_vec(out_dir / f"output_bias{suffix}", bias, dtype)

    manifest = {
        "state_len": state_len,
        "num_classes": num_classes,
        "hd1": hidden1,
        "hd2": hidden2,
        "nrd": residual_count,
        "output_dim": output_dim,
        "dtype": dtype,
        "normalization": "layernorm",
        "layout": "row-major input activations times weight_hxk",
        "embedding": "folded into position-class input table",
        "source_weights": str(weights_path),
    }
    (out_dir / "manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(
        "stream1_export_done"
        f" out_dir={out_dir}"
        f" format=resmlp_layernorm"
        f" dtype={dtype}"
        f" hd1={hidden1}"
        f" hd2={hidden2}"
        f" nrd={residual_count}"
        f" output_dim={output_dim}"
    )


def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--weights", required=True, type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("--out", required=True, type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("--format", choices=["batchnorm-folded", "resmlp-layernorm"], default="batchnorm-folded")
    parser.add_argument("--dtype", choices=["fp16", "bf16"], default="fp16")
    parser.add_argument("--num-classes", type=int, default=120)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.format == "batchnorm-folded":
        export_batchnorm_folded(args.weights, args.out, args.dtype, args.num_classes)
    else:
        export_resmlp_layernorm(args.weights, args.out, args.dtype)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

### Some utilities ###

def parse_initial_state(initial_state_str: str) -> np.ndarray:
    """Parse a comma-separated puzzle state string into an integer array."""
    return np.array([int(x) for x in initial_state_str.split(",")], dtype=int)


def parse_path(path_str: str) -> list[str]:
    """Parse a dot-separated generator path string into generator names."""
    return list(path_str.split("."))


def check_path(
    path_to_check: Sequence[str],
    initial_state: np.ndarray,
    central_state: np.ndarray,
    generators: dict[str, np.ndarray],
) -> bool:
    """Return whether applying a sequence of generator names reaches the central state."""
    state = initial_state.copy()
    for gen_name in path_to_check:
        gen = generators[gen_name]
        state = state[gen]
    return bool(np.all(state == central_state))


### Loading competition inputs ###

std::vector<std::string> load_p900_move_names(const std::filesystem::path& path) {
    const std::string text = read_text_file(path);
    std::size_t pos = text.find("\"names\"");
    const bool names_format = pos != std::string::npos;
    if (names_format) {
        pos = text.find('[', pos);
        if (pos == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_error("generator json malformed names");
        }
    } else {
        pos = text.find("\"generators\"");
        if (pos == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_erro<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="44" />      }
    }
    std::vector<std::string> names;
    while (names.size() < MOVE_COUNT) {
        const std::size_t begin = text.find('"', pos + 1);
        if (begin == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_error("p900 generator json missing move name");
        }
        const std::size_t end = text.find('"', begin + 1);
        if (end == std::string::npos) {
            throw std::runtime_error("p900 generator json malformed move name");
        }
        names.push_back(text.substr(begin + 1, end - begin - 1));
        pos = end;
        if (!names_format) {
            const std::size_t colon = text.find(':', pos);
            const std::size_t array = text.find('[', pos);
            if (colon == std::string::npos || array == std::string::npos || colon > array) {
                throw std::runtime_error("generator json malformed generator entry");
            }
            pos = array;
        }
    }
    return names;
}

State128 load_central_state(const std::filesystem::path& path) {
    const std::string text = read_text_file(path);
    std::size_t pos = text.find("\"central_state\"");
    if (pos == std::string::npos) {
        throw std::runtime_error("puzzle info json missing central_state");
    }
    pos = text.find('[', pos);
    if (pos == std::string::npos) {
        throw std::runtime_error("puzzle info json malformed central_state");
    }
    State128 state{};
    for (std::uint32_t p = 0; p < STATE_LEN; ++p) {
        state.v[p] = static_cast<std::uint8_t>(parse_next_u32(text, pos, "central_state"));
    }
    for (std::uint32_t p = STATE_LEN; p < STATE_STORAGE_LEN; ++p) {
        state.v[p] = 0;
    }
    return state;
}

class PilgrimAttnRes(nn.Module):
    """MLP distance model with optional residual or attention-residual blocks."""

    def __init__(self, config: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.dtype = torch.float32
        self.state_size = config['state_size']
        self.num_classes = config['num_classes']
        self.hd1 = config['hd1']
        self.hd2 = config['hd2']
        self.nrd = config['nrd']
        self.n_attn_blocks = config.get('n_attn_blocks', 0)
        self.block_type = config.get('block_type', 'residual')
        self.z_add = 0
        self.output_dim = 1

        state_size = self.state_size
        num_classes = self.num_classes
        hd1 = self.hd1
        hd2 = self.hd2
        dropout_rate = config['dropout_rate']

        self.input_layer = nn.Linear(state_size * num_classes, hd1)
        self.bn1 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hd1)
        self.relu = nn.ReLU()
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout_rate)

        if hd2 > 0:
            self.hidden_layer = nn.Linear(hd1, hd2)
            self.bn2 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hd2)
            hidden_dim_for_output = hd2
        else:
            self.hidden_layer = None
            self.bn2 = None
            hidden_dim_for_output = hd1

        if self.block_type == 'residual':
            self.residual_blocks = nn.ModuleList(
                [ResidualBlock(hd2, dropout_rate) for _ in range(self.nrd)]
            ) if self.nrd > 0 and hd2 > 0 else None
        elif self.block_type == 'attn_res':
            self.residual_blocks = nn.ModuleList(
                [AttnResidualBlock(hd2, dropout_rate) for _ in range(self.n_attn_blocks)]
            ) if self.n_attn_blocks > 0 and hd2 > 0 else None
        else:
            raise ValueError(f"Unknown block_type: {self.block_type}")

        self.output_layer = nn.Linear(hidden_dim_for_output, self.output_dim)

    def forward(self, z: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        """Predict distances for encoded puzzle states."""
        x = F.one_hot(z.long() + self.z_add, num_classes=self.num_classes).view(z.size(0), -1).to(self.dtype)

        x = self.input_layer(x)
        x = self.bn1(x)
        x = self.relu(x)
        x = self.dropout(x)

        if self.hidden_layer is not None:
            x = self.hidden_layer(x)
            x = self.bn2(x)
            x = self.relu(x)
            x = self.dropout(x)

        if self.residual_blocks is not None:
            if self.block_type == 'residual':
                for block in self.residual_blocks:
                    x = block(x)
            elif self.block_type == 'attn_res':
                prev_outputs = []
                for block in self.residual_blocks:
                    x = block(x, prev_outputs)
                    prev_outputs.append(x)

        x = self.output_layer(x)
        return x.flatten()


class ResidualBlock(nn.Module):
    """Two-layer residual MLP block with batch normalization and dropout."""

    def __init__(self, hidden_dim: int, dropout_rate: float = 0.1) -> None:
        super().__init__()
        self.fc1 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim)
        self.bn1 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hidden_dim)
        self.relu = nn.ReLU()
        self.dropout = nn.Dropout(dropout_rate)
        self.fc2 = nn.Linear(hidden_dim, hidden_dim)
        self.bn2 = nn.BatchNorm1d(hidden_dim)

    def forward(self, x: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        """Apply a residual feed-forward update to a hidden representation."""
        residual = x
        out = self.fc1(x)
        out = self.bn1(out)
        out = self.relu(out)
        out = self.dropout(out)
        out = self.fc2(out)
        out = self.bn2(out)
        out = out + residual
        out = self.relu(out)
        return out

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Export Stream1 PyTorch weights into runtime weight files."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
from typing import Dict, Literal, Tuple

import torch


TensorDict = Dict[str, torch.Tensor]
ExportDType = Literal["fp16", "bf16"]


def unwrap_state_dict(obj) -> TensorDict:
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        for key in ("state_dict", "model_state_dict", "model", "net", "module"):
            value = obj.get(key)
            if isinstance(value, dict):
                return value
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return obj
    raise TypeError(f"unsupported checkpoint root type: {type(obj).__name__}")


def strip_orig_mod(sd: TensorDict) -> TensorDict:
    return {(key[len("_orig_mod."):] if key.startswith("_orig_mod.") else key): value for key, value in sd.items()}


def fold_linear_bn(sd: TensorDict, linear: str, bn: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    gamma = sd[f"{bn}.weight"].detach().float()
    beta = sd[f"{bn}.bias"].detach().float()
    mean = sd[f"{bn}.running_mean"].detach().float()
    var = sd[f"{bn}.running_var"].detach().float()
    scale = gamma / torch.sqrt(var + 1e-5)
    return weight * scale[:, None], (bias - mean) * scale + beta


def linear_weight_bias(sd: TensorDict, linear: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    return weight, bias


def tensor_bytes(tensor: torch.Tensor, dty<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1319" />ed as position-class table",
        "source_weights": str(weights_path),
    }
    (out_dir / "manifest.json").write_text(json.dumps(manifest, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
    print(
        "stream1_export_done"
        f" out_dir={out_dir}"
        f" hd1={hd1_padded}"
        f" hd2={hd2_padded}"
        f" original_hd1={hd1}"
        f" original_hd2={hd2}"
        f" nrd={residual_count}"
        f" output_dim={output_dim}"
    )


def infer_resmlp_hidden_dims(sd: TensorDict) -> tuple[int, int, int, int, int]:
    embed_rows, embed_dim = sd["embedding.weight"].shape
    hidden1, flattened = sd["input_stack.0.weight"].shape
    hidden2, hidden1_in = sd["input_stack.3.weight"].shape
    output_dim, output_in = sd["head.weight"].shape
    if hidden1_in != hidden1 or output_in != hidden2:
        raise ValueError("ResMLPDistance hidden shape mismatch")
    if flattened % embed_dim != 0:
        raise ValueError(f"flattened input {flattened} is not divisible by embed_dim {embed_dim}")
    return int(embed_rows), int(embed_dim), int(hidden1), int(hidden2), int(output_dim)


def infer_resmlp_residual_count(sd: TensorDict) -> int:
    blocks = set()
    pattern = re.compile(r"^res_blocks\.(\d+)\.lin1\.weight$")
    for key in sd:
        match = pattern.match(key)
        if match:
            blocks.add(int(match.group(1)))
    if not blocks:
        raise ValueError("no res_blocks.*.lin1.weight tensors found")
    expected = set(range(max(blocks) + 1))
    if blocks != expected:
        raise ValueError(f"res block indices are not contiguous: found={sorted(blocks)}")
    return max(blocks) + 1


def linear_ln(sd: TensorDict, linear: str, ln: str) -> tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight, bias = linear_weight_bias(sd, linear)
    gamma = sd[f"{ln}.weight"].detach().float()
    beta = sd[f"{ln}.bias"].detach().float()
    return weight, bias, gamma, beta

def main() -> None:
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    parser.add_argument("--weights", required=True, type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("--out", required=True, type=Path)
    parser.add_argument("--format", choices=["batchnorm-folded", "resmlp-layernorm"], default="batchnorm-folded")
    parser.add_argument("--dtype", choices=["fp16", "bf16"], default="fp16")
    parser.add_argument("--num-classes", type=int, default=120)
    args = parser.parse_args()
    if args.format == "batchnorm-folded":
        export_batchnorm_folded(args.weights, args.out, args.dtype, args.num_classes)
    else:
        export_resmlp_layernorm(args.weights, args.out, args.dtype)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

- `B_MICRO=24,576`, ring/concurrency `2`: full depth `128.24s`, весь проход `172.30s`.
- `49,152`, ring/concurrency `4`: full depth `127.65s` — лучший steady-state.

*** Add File: test_results/kaggle_tetraminx_multigpu_2026-07-24.md
+# Kaggle Tetraminx MultiGPU integration
+
+## Confirmed 2xT4 baseline
+
+- Kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-gpu-t4-torchrun`, version 1.
+- Real topology: one node, two T4 ranks launched with torchrun.
+- Global beam: `2**24`; local frontier at full beam: `8,388,608` per rank.
+- Both ranks completed depths 0 through 9 without a runtime failure.
+- Runtime: approximately `89.23s`.
+- Logs: `test_results/kaggle_t4_torchrun_v1/`.
+
+## Output-24 tuning
+
+- Stable selection: `B_MICRO=2048`, Stream1 concurrency/ring `4`,
+  `SHARD_COUNT=8`, Stream4 batch/trigger `98,304`, active sort slots `4`.
+- Full run at global beam `2**22`, depth 7: approximately `8.17s`.
+- Stream4 batch/trigger `131,072` produced
+  `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` (code 3002) at depth 5.
+- Logs: `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_sweep_v5/`.
+
+## Output-1 tuning
+
+- Attached model: public Megaminx `2048x512x8`, output dimension 1.
+- Global beam `2**22`, depth 7, `SHARD_COUNT=8`, Stream4 batch/trigger `98,304`.
+- Row budget `24,576`: full depth `128.24s`, total `172.30s`.
+- Row budget `49,152`: full depth `127.65s`, total `179.65s`.
+- Row budget `98,304`: full depth `127.75s`, total `179.58s`.
+- Selected production value: `B_MICRO=49,152`, concurrency/ring `4`, based on
+  the best steady-state full-beam depth.
+- Logs: `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_sweep_v6/`.
+
+## Integration
+
+- Generated package: `kaggle_tetraminx_multigpu/`.
+- The source notebook's training and EMA/SWA selection are preserved.
+- The inference model is exported with `batchnorm-folded`, output dimension 1.
+- Solver build consumes the competition `puzzle_info.json`; runtime consumes
+  the same generator file and competition `test.csv`.
+- The original CayleyPy beam-search cell is replaced by a real two-rank
+  torchrun loop and successful shorter paths are merged into `submission.csv`.
+
+## Current blocker
+
+Kaggle rejected the private integrated push before execution:
+
+`You must accept this competition's rules before you'll be able to add it as a datasource`
+
+The `trydotatwo` account must accept the rules for
+`cayley-py-professor-tetraminx-solve-optimally`. After that, the prepared
+package can be pushed without further code changes.
*** End Patch

Do not publish publicly, do not alter CUDA/C++ algorithms merely to diagnose, and preserve unrelated dirty changes. Once successful, verify output paths/submission and report exact kernel version, timings, logs, and selected parameters.

# Work With Kaggle

Use this skill whenever the task involves Kaggle notebooks, kernels, datasets, or GPU validation runs.

## Project Memory First

For project repositories, read local project guidance before changing notebooks or source:

- `AGENTS.md`
- `memory/PROMPTS.md` and `memory/CHANGELOG.md`, especially recent Kaggle entries
- relevant `test_results/kaggle_*` reports for the same failure/config family

This project has many Kaggle-specific decisions preserved in memory. Do not assume a generic Kaggle workflow is correct when project memory says otherwise.

## Safety And Scope

- Do not upload or publish current uncommitted source to a Kaggle dataset unless the user explicitly approves external export.
- Prefer GitHub-backed Kaggle source when the user has selected GitHub as source of truth.
- Do not push a public Kaggle notebook/kernel unless the user explicitly approves publication or public push.
- Do not make C++/CUDA algorithmic changes while diagnosing Kaggle failures unless the user explicitly approves code changes. Config-only notebook changes are acceptable when useful and consistent with the request.
- Do not revert unrelated local changes. Kaggle notebook edits often coexist with dirty source changes.
- For multi-rank validation, do not fake `WORLD_SIZE=2` on one GPU with both ranks on `LOCAL_RANK=0` unless the user explicitly approves that limited <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="669" />stop the run and inspect downloaded logs. This rule came from project memory and applies to diagnostic/watch tasks.
- Do not stop production/no-timeout runs unless the user explicitly asks or the notebook itself has a configured timeout.
- If a run appears hung because stdout is redirected, check rank log files and whether live-rank streaming is enabled before assuming a stall.
- Preserve logs even when stopping: terminate gracefully when possible, then download output files.

## Failure Diagnosis Checklist

After a Kaggle failure, collect and scan:

- Kaggle worker status and kernel version
- notebook stdout tail
- rank logs under the notebook output directory
- result JSONL/CSV summaries
- GPU budget/preflight output
- explicit Kaggle messages such as disk quota failures

Common patterns in this project:

- `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` plus "more disk space than is available" usually means disk quota/static history arena pressure.
- `return_code=-9` can indicate host RAM OOM.
- `code=3002` / `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` points to Stream3/Stream4/threshold/capacity flow diagnostics.
- `final selected count does not match score phase counts` points to final selection/histogram/exact-count mismatch.
- `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` may require config-only headroom/shard-capacity changes before deeper code work.
- Silent output after build may simply mean rank stdout is redirected and live rank logging is disabled.

Write a short report under `test_results/` for meaningful Kaggle validations, sweeps, failures, or launches.

## Response Style

- Be explicit about whether you changed source code, notebook config only, or only launched/monitored a run.
- Include Kaggle kernel slug/version/status when known.
- Include exact local report/log paths in `test_results/`.
- Keep recommendations tied to evidence from logs, not guesses.
- If the next step requires exporting source or publishing publicly, ask for explicit approval first.

===== AGENTS.md =====
# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Export Stream1 PyTorch weights into runtime weight files."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
from typing import Dict, Literal, Tuple

import torch


TensorDict = Dict[str, torch.Tensor]
ExportDType = Literal["fp16", "bf16"]


def unwrap_state_dict(obj) -> TensorDict:
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        for key in ("state_dict", "model_state_dict", "model", "net", "module"):
            value = obj.get(key)
            if isinstance(value, dict):
                return value
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return obj
    raise TypeError(f"unsupported checkpoint root type: {type(obj).__name__}")


def strip_orig_mod(sd: TensorDict) -> TensorDict:
    return {(key[len("_orig_mod."):] if key.startswith("_orig_mod.") else key): value for key, value in sd.items()}


def fold_linear_bn(sd: TensorDict, linear: str, bn: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    gamma = sd[f"{bn}.weight"].detach().float()
    beta = sd[f"{bn}.bias"].detach().float()
    mean = sd[f"{bn}.running_mean"].detach().float()
    var = sd[f"{bn}.running_var"].detach().float()
    scale = gamma / torch.sqrt(var + 1e-5)
    return weight * scale[:, None], (bias - mean) * scale + beta


def linear_weight_bias(sd: TensorDict, linear: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    return weight, bias

[33] assistant: Верно, но точнее контракт такой:

- Поддерживаются только две уже реализованные схемы checkpoint:
  - `PilgrimAttnRes`/BatchNorm MLP — формат `batchnorm-folded`;
  - `ResMLPDistance`/LayerNorm MLP — формат `resmlp-layernorm`.
- `output_dim` определяется автоматически из весов.
- Solver принимает:
  - `output_dim=1`;
  - `output_dim=move_count` — для Megaminx это `24`.
- Произвольная PyTorch-архитектура не поддерживается.

Предлагаю три варианта:

1. Один универсальный notebook с автоматическим выбором профиля — рекомендую. Пользователь задаёт checkpoint, формат exporter, puzzle files, beam и depth. Notebook проверяет архитектуру/output_dim, выбирает профиль `2**16…2**25`, показывает итоговую конфигурацию и запускает 2×T4.

2. Два notebook: отдельно output-1 и output-24. Проще внутри, но настройки быстро разойдутся и пользователям придётся выбирать правильный файл.

3. Notebook-конструктор генерирует специализированный notebook. Гибко, но лишний уровень сложности для Kaggle-пользователя.

Дизайн рекомендованного варианта:

- В самом верху — крупный блок `SUPPORTED MODELS`, где прямо перечислены две схемы checkpoint, допустимые output dimensions и пример замены файла весов.
- Одна короткая config-cell: модель, exporter format, puzzle files, `BEAM_POWER`, depth, puzzle ids и режим benchmark/solve.
- Таблица профилей отдельно для output-1 и output-24, по каждой степени `16…25`.
- Профиль выбирается не только по beam: учитываются output dimension, число ходов и фактический MLP row budget.
- Перед запуском — fail-fast проверка checkpoint, manifest, `output_dim`, двух T4, памяти и рассчитанных capacity.
- Тюнинг оценивает steady-state время полной глубины, throughput и VRAM; решение конкретной головоломки не используется как единственная метрика.
- Для каждого beam выбирается самый быстрый стабильный профиль с запасом памяти; OOM, overflow и нестабильные точки сохраняются в отчёте.
- Финальные артефакты: CSV sweep, выбранные profiles JSON, rank logs, results CSV и submission.

Если этот дизайн устраивает, ответь «да» — зафиксирую spec и перейду к реализации и реальным 2×T4 sweep.

[35] assistant: Понял. Реальный `beam_width` всегда остаётся пользовательским; профиль даёт только параметры исполнения. Solver затем делает своё небольшое выравнивание beam под `world_size × shards × alignment`.

Осталось определить правило «ближайшего» профиля. Рекомендую сравнивать в логарифмической шкале:

```python
profile_power = round(log2(user_beam_width))
profile_power = clamp(profile_power, 16, 25)
```

Это естественно для профилей `2**N`: граница между соседними профилями будет по геометрическому среднему, а не по абсолютному числу состояний. Сам beam при этом не заменяется на `2**profile_power`.

Используем такое правило?

+# Kaggle 2xT4 MLP Beam Autoprofiles
+
+## Goal
+
+Provide one reusable private Kaggle notebook for real 2xT4
+`MultiGPUBeamSearch` runs. A user supplies a supported MLP checkpoint, puzzle
+inputs, an exact beam width, depth, and puzzle ids. The notebook validates the
+inputs, exports the model, selects the nearest measured runtime profile, builds
+the solver, runs two ranks, and preserves logs and result artifacts.
+
+The notebook must make `2**25` practical enough to benchmark and use. It must
+also provide measured profiles anchored at every power from `2**16` through
+`2**25`, separately for one-output and per-move-output MLPs.
+
+## Supported Model Contract
+
+The notebook header must state that arbitrary PyTorch models are not supported.
+It accepts only checkpoint layouts already implemented by
+`tools/export_stream1_mlp.py`:
+
+1. `PilgrimAttnRes`-style BatchNorm MLP checkpoints exported as
+   `batchnorm-folded`.
+2. `ResMLPDistance`-style LayerNorm MLP checkpoints exported as
+   `resmlp-layernorm`.
+
+The exporter infers `output_dim` from the checkpoint. The solver accepts:
+
+- `output_dim == 1`; or
+- `output_dim == move_count`, which is `24` for Megaminx.
+
+Every other model layout or output dimension fails before build or solver
+launch with an actionable message.
+
+## User Interface
+
+The first notebook section contains:
+
+- a prominent `SUPPORTED MODELS` markdown block with the exact contract above;
+- one config cell containing checkpoint path, exporter format, export dtype,
+  puzzle-info path, test CSV path, exact beam width, maximum depth, puzzle ids,
+  and benchmark/solve mode;
+- optional advanced overrides kept in a separate collapsed section.
+
+The ordinary path requires no editing below the first config cell.
+
+## Profile Selection
+
+Profiles are keyed by:
+
+- hardware: Kaggle `2xT4`;
+- model class: `output1` o<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1271" />exercised;
+- both supported output modes complete a real two-rank Kaggle smoke;
+- representative small, middle, and `2**25` anchors complete on 2xT4;
+- all profile rows have evidence or are explicitly marked unvalidated;
+- output paths, logs, results, and submission are downloaded and inspected;
+- the notebook remains private until the user separately approves publication.

Validate exactly two T4 GPUs and explicit torchrun topology.\n+2. Identify and export the checkpoint.\n+3. Validate manifest state dimensions, class count, move count, and output mode.\n+4. Select and display the nearest profile.\n+5. Compute aligned/local beam, capacities, history budget, disk need, and\n+   expected GPU allocation.\n+6. Fail closed on invalid or unsafe budgets.\n+7. Clone and build transient dependencies under `/tmp`.\n+8. Launch the existing C++ runner through explicit two-rank torchrun.\n+9. Stream concise rank-zero progress while preserving full rank logs.\n+10. Write machine-readable results under `/kaggle/working`.\n+\n+Notebook tuning does not change CUDA/C++ search algorithms.\n+\n+## Errors and Diagnostics\n+\n+The notebook distinguishes unsupported checkpoints, unsupported output\n+dimensions, model/generator mismatches, wrong hardware, failed preflight,\n+invalid runtime configuration, stream overflow, CUDA graph allocation failure,\n+rank failure, and unsolved-within-depth.\n+\n+Failures preserve logs, selected profile, derived configuration, and a summary\n+JSON. They do not silently clamp capacities, change the user's beam, or fall\n+back to another model mode.\n+\n+## Artifacts\n+\n+The tuning kernel produces raw logs, a CSV of every attempted configuration, a\n+JSON file with selected profiles, and a Markdown verification report. The user\n+notebook produces the exported manifest, requested/effective beam metadata,\n+selected profile snapshot, per-puzzle results, rank logs, final summary JSON,\n+and a submission CSV in solve mode.\n+\n+## Verification Gates\n+\n+- Notebook JSON parses and ordinary Python cells AST-parse.\n+- Profile selection tests cover boundaries, midpoints, clamps, and arbitrary\n+  non-power-of-two beams.\n+- The requested beam is preserved and only altered by documented alignment.\n+- Both exporter formats are locally exercised.\n+- Both output modes complete a real two-rank Kaggle smoke.\n+- Representative small, middle, and `2**25` anchors complete on 2xT4.\n+- Every profile has evidence or is explicitly marked unvalidated.\n+- Downloaded logs, results, paths, and submission are inspected.\n+- The notebook remains private until publication is separately approved.\n*** End Patch",

# Work With Kaggle

Use this skill whenever the task involves Kaggle notebooks, kernels, datasets, or GPU validation runs.

## Project Memory First

For project repositories, read local project guidance before changing notebooks or source:

- `AGENTS.md`
- `memory/PROMPTS.md` and `memory/CHANGELOG.md`, especially recent Kaggle entries
- relevant `test_results/kaggle_*` reports for the same failure/config family

This project has many Kaggle-specific decisions preserved in memory. Do not assume a generic Kaggle workflow is correct when project memory says otherwise.

## Safety And Scope

- Do not upload or publish current uncommitted source to a Kaggle dataset unless the user explicitly approves external export.
- Prefer GitHub-backed Kaggle source when the user has selected GitHub as source of truth.
- Do not push a public Kaggle notebook/kernel unless the user explicitly approves publication or public push.
- Do not make C++/CUDA algorithmic changes while diagnosing Kaggle failures unless the user explicitly approves code changes. Config-only notebook changes are acceptable when useful and consistent with the request.
- Do not revert unrelated local changes. Kaggle notebook edits often coexist with dirty source changes.
- For multi-rank validation, do not fake `WORLD_SIZE=2` on one GPU with both ranks on `LOCAL_RANK=0` unless the user explicitly approves that limited <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="669" />stop the run and inspect downloaded logs. This rule came from project memory and applies to diagnostic/watch tasks.
- Do not stop production/no-timeout runs unless the user explicitly asks or the notebook itself has a configured timeout.
- If a run appears hung because stdout is redirected, check rank log files and whether live-rank streaming is enabled before assuming a stall.
- Preserve logs even when stopping: terminate gracefully when possible, then download output files.

## Failure Diagnosis Checklist

After a Kaggle failure, collect and scan:

- Kaggle worker status and kernel version
- notebook stdout tail
- rank logs under the notebook output directory
- result JSONL/CSV summaries
- GPU budget/preflight output
- explicit Kaggle messages such as disk quota failures

Common patterns in this project:

- `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` plus "more disk space than is available" usually means disk quota/static history arena pressure.
- `return_code=-9` can indicate host RAM OOM.
- `code=3002` / `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` points to Stream3/Stream4/threshold/capacity flow diagnostics.
- `final selected count does not match score phase counts` points to final selection/histogram/exact-count mismatch.
- `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` may require config-only headroom/shard-capacity changes before deeper code work.
- Silent output after build may simply mean rank stdout is redirected and live rank logging is disabled.

Write a short report under `test_results/` for meaningful Kaggle validations, sweeps, failures, or launches.

## Response Style

- Be explicit about whether you changed source code, notebook config only, or only launched/monitored a run.
- Include Kaggle kernel slug/version/status when known.
- Include exact local report/log paths in `test_results/`.
- Keep recommendations tied to evidence from logs, not guesses.
- If the next step requires exporting source or publishing publicly, ask for explicit approval first.

===== AGENTS.md =====
# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Export Stream1 PyTorch weights into runtime weight files."""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
from typing import Dict, Literal, Tuple

import torch


TensorDict = Dict[str, torch.Tensor]
ExportDType = Literal["fp16", "bf16"]


def unwrap_state_dict(obj) -> TensorDict:
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        for key in ("state_dict", "model_state_dict", "model", "net", "module"):
            value = obj.get(key)
            if isinstance(value, dict):
                return value
    if isinstance(obj, dict):
        return obj
    raise TypeError(f"unsupported checkpoint root type: {type(obj).__name__}")


def strip_orig_mod(sd: TensorDict) -> TensorDict:
    return {(key[len("_orig_mod."):] if key.startswith("_orig_mod.") else key): value for key, value in sd.items()}


def fold_linear_bn(sd: TensorDict, linear: str, bn: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    gamma = sd[f"{bn}.weight"].detach().float()
    beta = sd[f"{bn}.bias"].detach().float()
    mean = sd[f"{bn}.running_mean"].detach().float()
    var = sd[f"{bn}.running_var"].detach().float()
    scale = gamma / torch.sqrt(var + 1e-5)
    return weight * scale[:, None], (bias - mean) * scale + beta


def linear_weight_bias(sd: TensorDict, linear: str) -> Tuple[torch.Tensor, torch.Tensor]:
    weight = sd[f"{linear}.weight"].detach().float()
    bias = sd.get(f"{linear}.bias")
    if bias is None:
        bias = torch.zeros(weight.shape[0], dtype=torch.float32)
    else:
        bias = bias.detach().float()
    return weight, bias

[33] assistant: Верно, но точнее контракт такой:

- Поддерживаются только две уже реализованные схемы checkpoint:
  - `PilgrimAttnRes`/BatchNorm MLP — формат `batchnorm-folded`;
  - `ResMLPDistance`/LayerNorm MLP — формат `resmlp-layernorm`.
- `output_dim` определяется автоматически из весов.
- Solver принимает:
  - `output_dim=1`;
  - `output_dim=move_count` — для Megaminx это `24`.
- Произвольная PyTorch-архитектура не поддерживается.

Предлагаю три варианта:

1. Один универсальный notebook с автоматическим выбором профиля — рекомендую. Пользователь задаёт checkpoint, формат exporter, puzzle files, beam и depth. Notebook проверяет архитектуру/output_dim, выбирает профиль `2**16…2**25`, показывает итоговую конфигурацию и запускает 2×T4.

2. Два notebook: отдельно output-1 и output-24. Проще внутри, но настройки быстро разойдутся и пользователям придётся выбирать правильный файл.

3. Notebook-конструктор генерирует специализированный notebook. Гибко, но лишний уровень сложности для Kaggle-пользователя.

Дизайн рекомендованного варианта:

- В самом верху — крупный блок `SUPPORTED MODELS`, где прямо перечислены две схемы checkpoint, допустимые output dimensions и пример замены файла весов.
- Одна короткая config-cell: модель, exporter format, puzzle files, `BEAM_POWER`, depth, puzzle ids и режим benchmark/solve.
- Таблица профилей отдельно для output-1 и output-24, по каждой степени `16…25`.
- Профиль выбирается не только по beam: учитываются output dimension, число ходов и фактический MLP row budget.
- Перед запуском — fail-fast проверка checkpoint, manifest, `output_dim`, двух T4, памяти и рассчитанных capacity.
- Тюнинг оценивает steady-state время полной глубины, throughput и VRAM; решение конкретной головоломки не используется как единственная метрика.
- Для каждого beam выбирается самый быстрый стабильный профиль с запасом памяти; OOM, overflow и нестабильные точки сохраняются в отчёте.
- Финальные артефакты: CSV sweep, выбранные profiles JSON, rank logs, results CSV и submission.

Если этот дизайн устраивает, ответь «да» — зафиксирую spec и перейду к реализации и реальным 2×T4 sweep.

[35] assistant: Понял. Реальный `beam_width` всегда остаётся пользовательским; профиль даёт только параметры исполнения. Solver затем делает своё небольшое выравнивание beam под `world_size × shards × alignment`.

Осталось определить правило «ближайшего» профиля. Рекомендую сравнивать в логарифмической шкале:

```python
profile_power = round(log2(user_beam_width))
profile_power = clamp(profile_power, 16, 25)
```

Это естественно для профилей `2**N`: граница между соседними профилями будет по геометрическому среднему, а не по абсолютному числу состояний. Сам beam при этом не заменяется на `2**profile_power`.

Используем такое правило?

+# Kaggle 2xT4 MLP Beam Autoprofiles
+
+## Goal
+
+Provide one reusable private Kaggle notebook for real 2xT4
+`MultiGPUBeamSearch` runs. A user supplies a supported MLP checkpoint, puzzle
+inputs, an exact beam width, depth, and puzzle ids. The notebook validates the
+inputs, exports the model, selects the nearest measured runtime profile, builds
+the solver, runs two ranks, and preserves logs and result artifacts.
+
+The notebook must make `2**25` practical enough to benchmark and use. It must
+also provide measured profiles anchored at every power from `2**16` through
+`2**25`, separately for one-output and per-move-output MLPs.
+
+## Supported Model Contract
+
+The notebook header must state that arbitrary PyTorch models are not supported.
+It accepts only checkpoint layouts already implemented by
+`tools/export_stream1_mlp.py`:
+
+1. `PilgrimAttnRes`-style BatchNorm MLP checkpoints exported as
+   `batchnorm-folded`.
+2. `ResMLPDistance`-style LayerNorm MLP checkpoints exported as
+   `resmlp-layernorm`.
+
+The exporter infers `output_dim` from the checkpoint. The solver accepts:
+
+- `output_dim == 1`; or
+- `output_dim == move_count`, which is `24` for Megaminx.
+
+Every other model layout or output dimension fails before build or solver
+launch with an actionable message.
+
+## User Interface
+
+The first notebook section contains:
+
+- a prominent `SUPPORTED MODELS` markdown block with the exact contract above;
+- one config cell containing checkpoint path, exporter format, export dtype,
+  puzzle-info path, test CSV path, exact beam width, maximum depth, puzzle ids,
+  and benchmark/solve mode;
+- optional advanced overrides kept in a separate collapsed section.
+
+The ordinary path requires no editing below the first config cell.
+
+## Profile Selection
+
+Profiles are keyed by:
+
+- hardware: Kaggle `2xT4`;
+- model class: `output1` o<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1271" />exercised;
+- both supported output modes complete a real two-rank Kaggle smoke;
+- representative small, middle, and `2**25` anchors complete on 2xT4;
+- all profile rows have evidence or are explicitly marked unvalidated;
+- output paths, logs, results, and submission are downloaded and inspected;
+- the notebook remains private until the user separately approves publication.

+The notebook distinguishes unsupported checkpoints, unsupported output

  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-24-kaggle-t4-mlp-autoprofiles-design.md\n+# Kaggle 2xT4 MLP Beam Autoprofiles\n+\n+## Goal\n+\n+Provide one reusable private Kaggle notebook for real 2xT4\n+`MultiGPUBeamSearch` runs. A user supplies a supported MLP checkpoint, puzzle\n+inputs, an exact beam width, depth, and puzzle ids. The notebook validates the\n+inputs, exports the model, selects the nearest measured runtime profile, builds\n+the solver, runs two ranks, and preserves logs and result artifacts.\n+\n+The notebook must make `2**25` practical enough to benchmark and use. It must\n+also provide measured profiles anchored at every power from `2**16` through\n+`2**25`, separately for one-output and per-move-output MLPs.\n+\n+## Supported Model Contract\n+\n+The notebook header must state that arbitrary PyTorch models are not supported.\n+It accepts only checkpoint layouts already implemented by\n+`tools/export_stream1_mlp.py`:\n+\n+1. `PilgrimAttnRes`-style BatchNorm MLP checkpoints exported as\n+   `batchnorm-folded`.\n+2. `ResMLPDistance`-style LayerNorm MLP checkpoints exported as\n+   `resmlp-layernorm`.\n+\n+The exporter infers `output_dim` from the checkpoint. The solver accepts\n+`output_dim == 1` or `output_dim == move_count`, which is `24` for Megaminx.\n+Every other model layout or output dimension fails before build or solver\n+launch with an actionable message.\n+\n+## User Interface\n+\n+The first notebook section contains:\n+\n+- a prominent `SUPPORTED MODELS` block with the exact contract above;\n+- one config cell containing checkpoint path, exporter format, export dtype,\n+  puzzle-info path, test CSV path, exact beam width, maximum depth, puzzle ids,\n+  and benchmark/solve mode;\n+- optional advanced overrides in a separate section.\n+\n+The ordinary path requires no editing below the first config cell.\n+\n+## Profile Selection\n+\n+Profiles are keyed by Kaggle `2xT4`, model class (`output1` or\n+`output_move_count`), and every anchor beam power from `16` through `25`.\n+\n+For a positive user beam width `B`, the notebook selects:\n+\n+```python\n+profile_power = clamp(round_half_up(log2(B)), 16, 25)\n+```\n+\n+Half-up rounding makes midpoint behavior deterministic. The selected profile\n+supplies runtime parameters only and never replaces the requested beam.\n+\n+The runner receives the exact user beam width. The existing solver alignment\n+rule may increase the effective beam slightly to satisfy\n+`world_size * shard_count * alignment`. The notebook prints requested beam,\n+profile anchor, effective beam, local beam, and alignment delta before launch.\n+\n+## Profile Contents\n+\n+Each profile records Stream1 row budget and concurrency, Stream3 ring slots,\n+shard count and capacity scale, Stream4 batch/trigger/sort slots, GPU headroom,\n+history settings, steady-state depth time, candidate throughput, static or peak\n+VRAM, status, and evidence kernel version.\n+\n+Profiles for `output_dim=1` and `output_dim=move_count` are independent. For\n+`output_dim=1`, row budgeting is converted using the generator move count; no\n+Megaminx-specific constant is hidden in the selector.\n+\n+## Tuning Method\n+\n+The sweep runs on real Kaggle 2xT4 hardware with fixed code revision, build\n+flags, model checkpoints, and puzzle workload.\n+\n+1. Establish a correctness and two-rank baseline.\n+2. Measure anchors `2**16..2**25` for both output classes.\n+3. Tune one family at a time: row budget/concurrency, shards/capacity, Stream3\n+   ring, then Stream4 batch/trigger/slots.\n+4. Warm up setup-heavy paths before steady-state measurement.\n+5. Record end-to-end and per-phase/depth time.\n+6. Preserve OOM, overflow, timeout, and invalid-config rows.\n+7. Select the fastest stable point with memory and capacity margin.\n+\n+The primary metric is steady-state time at a full frontier. Puzzle solve time\n+is reported but is not the selection metric because it depends on model quality\n+and puzzle difficulty. Adjacent small-beam anchors may share a configuration,\n+but each anchor still receives an explicit measured evidence row.\n+\n+## Notebook Execution Flow\n+\n+1. Validate exactly two T4 GPUs and explicit torchrun topology.\n+2. Identify and export the checkpoint.\n+3. Validate manifest state dimensions, class count, move count, and output mode.\n+4. Select and display the nearest profile.\n+5. Compute aligned/local beam, capacities, history budget, disk need, and\n+   expected GPU allocation.\n+6. Fail closed on invalid or unsafe budgets.\n+7. Clone and build transient dependencies under `/tmp`.\n+8. Launch the existing C++ runner through explicit two-rank torchrun.\n+9. Stream concise rank-zero progress while preserving full rank logs.\n+10. Write machine-readable results under `/kaggle/working`.\n+\n+Notebook tuning does not change CUDA/C++ search algorithms.\n+\n+## Errors and Diagnostics\n+\n+The notebook distinguishes unsupported checkpoints, unsupported output\n+dimensions, model/generator mismatches, wrong hardware, failed preflight,\n+invalid runtime configuration, stream overflow, CUDA graph allocation failure,\n+rank failure, and unsolved-within-depth.\n+\n+Failures preserve logs, selected profile, derived configuration, and a summary\n+JSON. They do not silently clamp capacities, change the user's beam, or fall\n+back to another model mode.\n+\n+## Artifacts\n+\n+The tuning kernel produces raw logs, a CSV of every attempted configuration, a\n+JSON file with selected profiles, and a Markdown verification report. The user\n+notebook produces the exported manifest, requested/effective beam metadata,\n+selected profile snapshot, per-puzzle results, rank logs, final summary JSON,\n+and a submission CSV in solve mode.\n+\n+## Verification Gates\n+\n+- Notebook JSON parses and ordinary Python cells AST-parse.\n+- Profile selection tests cover boundaries, midpoints, clamps, and arbitrary\n+  non-power-of-two beams.\n+- The requested beam is preserved and only altered by documented alignment.\n+- Both exporter formats are locally exercised.\n+- Both output modes complete a real two-rank Kaggle smoke.\n+- Representative small, middle, and `2**25` anchors complete on 2xT4.\n+- Every profile has evidence or is explicitly marked unvalidated.\n+- Downloaded logs, results, paths, and submission are inspected.\n+- The notebook remains private until publication is separately approved.\n*** End Patch",
  "tool": "apply_patch"
}
>>> APPROVAL REQUEST END

---
name: writing-plans
description: Use when you have a spec or requirements for a multi-step task, before touching code
---

# Writing Plans

## Overview

Write comprehensive implementation plans assuming the engineer has zero context for our codebase and questionable taste. Document everything they need to know: which files to touch for each task, code, testing, docs they might need to check, how to test it. Give them the whole plan as bite-sized tasks. DRY. YAGNI. TDD. Frequent commits.

Assume they are a skilled developer, but know almost nothing about our toolset or problem domain. Assume they don't know good test design very well.

**Announce at start:** "I'm using the writing-plans skill to create the implementation plan."

**Context:** If working in an isolated worktree, it should have been created via the `superpowers:using-git-worktrees` skill at execution time.

**Save plans to:** `docs/superpowers/plans/YYYY-MM-DD-<feature-name>.md`
- (User preferences for plan location override this default)

## Scope Check

If the spec covers multiple independent subsystems, it should have been broken into sub-project specs during brainstorming. If it wasn't, suggest breaking this into separate plans вЂ” one per subsystem. Each plan should produce working, testable software on its own.

## File Structure

Before defining tasks, map out which files will be created or modified and what each one is responsible for. This is where decomposition decisions get locked in.

- Design units with clear boundaries and well-defined interfaces. Each file should have one clear responsibility.
- You reason best about code you can hold in context at once, and your edits are more reliable when files are focused. Prefer smaller, focused files over large ones that do too much.
- Files that change together should live together. Split by responsibility, not by technical layer.
- In existing codebases, follow established pa<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="796" />st code)
- "Similar to Task N" (repeat the code вЂ” the engineer may be reading tasks out of order)
- Steps that describe what to do without showing how (code blocks required for code steps)
- References to types, functions, or methods not defined in any task

## Remember
- Exact file paths always
- Complete code in every step вЂ” if a step changes code, show the code
- Exact commands with expected output
- DRY, YAGNI, TDD, frequent commits

## Self-Review

After writing the complete plan, look at the spec with fresh eyes and check the plan against it. This is a checklist you run yourself вЂ” not a subagent dispatch.

**1. Spec coverage:** Skim each section/requirement in the spec. Can you point to a task that implements it? List any gaps.

**2. Placeholder scan:** Search your plan for red flags вЂ” any of the patterns from the "No Placeholders" section above. Fix them.

**3. Type consistency:** Do the types, method signatures, and property names you used in later tasks match what you defined in earlier tasks? A function called `clearLayers()` in Task 3 but `clearFullLayers()` in Task 7 is a bug.

If you find issues, fix them inline. No need to re-review вЂ” just fix and move on. If you find a spec requirement with no task, add the task.

## Execution Handoff

After saving the plan, offer execution choice:

**"Plan complete and saved to `docs/superpowers/plans/<filename>.md`. Two execution options:**

**1. Subagent-Driven (recommended)** - I dispatch a fresh subagent per task, review between tasks, fast iteration

**2. Inline Execution** - Execute tasks in this session using executing-plans, batch execution with checkpoints

**Which approach?"**

**If Subagent-Driven chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development
- Fresh subagent per task + two-stage review

**If Inline Execution chosen:**
- **REQUIRED SUB-SKILL:** Use superpowers:executing-plans
- Batch execution with checkpoints for review

+# Kaggle 2xT4 MLP Autoprofiles Implementation Plan
+
+> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.
+
+**Goal:** Build and validate one private Kaggle notebook that accepts an exact user beam, exports either supported MLP checkpoint family, and automatically selects independently measured 2xT4 profiles anchored at `2**16..2**25` for output-1 and output-move-count models.
+
+**Architecture:** Keep profile selection and validation in a small pure-Python module with deterministic unit tests. Generate the user notebook and the tuning kernel from versioned Python builders, record raw Kaggle evidence under `test_results/`, then freeze only stable measured configurations into a JSON profile registry consumed by the notebook.
+
+**Tech Stack:** Python 3, pytest, nbformat/JSON, PyTorch checkpoint inspection, Kaggle CLI, Kaggle 2xT4, CMake/Ninja, torchrun, existing CUDA/C++ `production_runner`.
+
+## Global Constraints
+
+- Support only `batchnorm-folded` PilgrimAttnRes-style and `resmlp-layernorm` ResMLPDistance-style checkpoints.
+- Accept only `output_dim == 1` or `output_dim == move_count`.
+- Select `profile_power = clamp(round_half_up(log2(B)), 16, 25)`.
+- Preserve the user's requested beam; only the existing `world_size * shard_count * alignment` rule may increase the effective beam.
+- Keep output-1 and output-move-count profiles independent.
+- Validate on real Kaggle 2xT4; never fake two ranks on one GPU.
+- Keep all notebooks private unless the user separately approves publication.
+- Do not change CUDA/C++ search algorithms as part of configuration tuning.
+- Preserve unrelated dirty worktree changes.
+- Store logs, outputs, and verification reports under `test_results/`.
+
+<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="3119" />reate: `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_universal_2026-07-24.md`
+- Create: `test_results/kaggle_t4_mlp_universal_v*/`
+- Modify: `memory/CHANGELOG.md`
+- Modify: `memory/PROMPTS.md`
+
+**Interfaces:**
+- Consumes: completed universal private notebook.
+- Produces: verified private Kaggle URL and exact usage/evidence report.
+
+- [ ] **Step 1: Push the universal notebook privately**
+
+Run: `kaggle kernels push -p kaggle_t4_mlp_universal`
+
+Expected: private kernel version launches on two T4 GPUs.
+
+- [ ] **Step 2: Smoke both output modes**
+
+Run one representative non-power-of-two requested beam for each output mode. Verify the selected anchor follows half-up `log2`, requested beam remains exact in runner arguments, effective beam changes only by alignment, and both ranks reach measured depths.
+
+- [ ] **Step 3: Download and inspect artifacts**

+git diff --check
+```
+
